[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU),
module name `tpu`, implementing the EXACT per-cycle behavior of the logikbench
`tpu` benchmark (zeroasiccorp/logikbench, MIT).

FUNCTION: computes C = A * B tile products on an 8x8 grid of processing
elements. B (the "weight" matrix, 8-bit signed) is loaded into the array and
held; A (the "activation" matrix, 8-bit signed) streams through; result rows
C (32-bit signed) drain out the bottom.

PROTOCOL:
  1. Weight load: assert w_valid for 8 cycles; w_data carries one weight row
     per cycle, 8 signed bytes packed { col 7 ... col 0 }, BOTTOM matrix row
     first (cycle k carries B[7-k][*]; the weights shift south). After 8
     cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
  2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle (packed the
     same way). Row m of the result, C[m][*] packed { col 7 ... col 0 } as 8
     signed 32-bit words, appears on c_data with c_valid high exactly 15
     (= 2*8-1) compute cycles after its A row was accepted.
  - The array advances only while w_valid is low: asserting w_valid stalls
    any in-flight computation (and reloads weights). Zeros are injected while
    a_valid is low, so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
  - Multiple A tiles may stream back-to-back against the same loaded weights.

CYCLE ACCURACY: c_valid is compared against the original design EVERY cycle;
c_data is compared on every cycle where c_valid is high. The 15-cycle
compute-latency and the stall semantics are part of the contract.

Ports:
  clk, rst (async active-high reset, asserted only before the stream begins)
  w_valid, w_data[63:0]
  a_valid, a_data[63:0]
  c_valid, c_data[255:0]

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` is the
original logikbench RTL (tpu_pe / tpu_array / tpu: PE mesh, per-row input
skew, per-column output de-skew, valid pipeline). It passes every cycle of the golden
trace in `vectors.dat` (directed zero/identity/extreme tiles, random tiles,
back-to-back streams, a_valid gaps, mid-drain weight reloads, random soak).
Study it, then optimize while preserving the cycle-accurate behavior. The
sibling benchmark `tpu_spire` shares the identical testbench and vectors
with a SpireHDL starting point.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[TOOL RESULT]
[Step 1/60]
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Processing element (PE) for the weight-stationary systolic array (TPU MXU).
//
// Each PE holds one stationary signed weight 'w'. Two independent datapaths
// share the cell:
//
//   Weight load (ld): the weights form a per-column shift register that loads
//   from the top. On each ld cycle the PE captures the weight from the PE above
//   (w_north) and presents its stored weight downward (w_south). After N load
//   cycles every PE in a column holds its final stationary weight.
//
//   Compute (en): the activation flows west to east (registered, one hop per
//   cycle) and the partial sum flows north to south, accumulating the local
//   product a_west * w. This is the only arithmetic cell in the array: one
//   signed DW x DW multiply plus an ACCW-bit add.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_pe
  #(parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator / partial-sum width (signed)
   (
    input		  clk,
    input		  rst,	      // synchronous, active high
    input		  ld,	      // weight-load shift enable
    input		  en,	      // compute enable
    input [DW-1:0]	  w_north,    // weight in from PE above
    input [DW-1:0]	  a_west,     // activation in from west
    input [ACCW-1:0]	  psum_north, // partial sum in from above
    output [DW-1:0]	  w_south,    // stored weight out to PE below
    output reg [DW-1:0]	  a_east,     // activation out to east
    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south  // partial sum out to below
    );

   // stationary weight register; presented downward for the load shift chain
   reg [DW-1:0] w;
   assign w_south = w;

   // local product: the ONLY signed operation. Operands are interpreted as
   // signed here via $signed; the 2*DW-bit result holds the two's-complement
   // product, which is then sign-extended to the accumulator width below.
   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);

   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst) begin
         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};
         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};
         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
      end
      else begin
         if (ld)
           w <= w_north;
         if (en) begin
            a_east     <= a_west;
            psum_south <= psum_north +
                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};
         end
      end
   end

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// N x N weight-stationary systolic array (the TPU MXU core).
//
// One tpu_pe per grid element, wired as a 2D mesh (per CLAUDE.md: replicated
// hardware via generate, never a procedural unroll):
//
//   - weights load from the top: w_top[j] feeds column j; each column is a
//     vertical shift register (w_south of PE(i,j) -> w_north of PE(i+1,j)).
//   - activations flow west to east: a_left[i] enters row i; a_east of PE(i,j)
//     feeds a_west of PE(i,j+1).
//   - partial sums flow north to south and accumulate; the top row's psum_north
//     is tied to zero (fresh accumulation), and c_bot[j] is the column-j result
//     leaving the bottom row.
//
// With weight B[i][j] held at PE(i,j) and activation A[m][i] entering row i,
// column j computes C[m][j] = sum_i A[m][i] * B[i][j]. Input/output skew (which
// time-aligns rows and columns) lives in the tpu top, not here.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_array
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N PEs)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	// synchronous, active high
    input		ld,	// weight-load shift enable
    input		en,	// compute enable
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_top,	// weight inputs, one per column (load)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_left,	// activation inputs, one per row
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_bot	// column results, one per column
    );

   // mesh nets: a_h carries activations east, psum_v carries sums south,
   // w_v carries weights south during load.
   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];
   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];
   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];

   genvar	   i, j;
   generate
      // west edge: activation inputs into column 0 of each row
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west
         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];
      end
      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out

         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];

//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU).
//
// Computes one tile product C = A * B, where A and B are N x N signed operands
// (DW bits) and C is N x N signed (ACCW bits). B is the "weight" matrix, loaded
// into and held by the array; A is the "activation" matrix, streamed through; C
// drains out the bottom. Built from a tpu_array of N x N tpu_pe cells.
//
// Operation has two phases the host drives via valids:
//
//   1. Weight load: assert w_valid for N cycles. w_data carries one weight row
//      per cycle, N signed weights packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }. Because the
//      array loads top-down (shift south), push the BOTTOM matrix row first:
//      cycle k carries B[N-1-k][*]. After N cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
//
//   2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle, a_data
//      packed { col N-1 ... col 0 } = A[m][N-1..0]. Row m of the result, C[m][*]
//      (packed the same way), appears on c_data with c_valid high, 2*N-1 cycles
//      after its A row was accepted.
//
// Time alignment: row i of A is delayed i cycles entering the array (input
// skew) so partial sums chain correctly down each column; column j of the
// result is delayed N-1-j cycles (output de-skew) so a full result row leaves
// together. Both skew networks and the valid pipeline advance only with the
// array (compute enable), so the 2*N-1 latency is exact. Per CLAUDE.md the skew
// chains are generated per-row/column shift registers, not a procedural unroll.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // result / accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	 // synchronous, active high
    input		w_valid, // weight-row valid (load phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_data,	 // weight row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    input		a_valid, // activation-row valid (compute phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_data,	 // activation row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    output		c_valid, // result-row valid
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_data	 // result row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    );

   // total injection-to-output latency, in compute cycles
   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;

   // load weights while w_valid; otherwise the array computes (and drains)
   wire	      ld = w_valid;
   wire	      en = ~w_valid;

   //##########################################################################
   // Input skew: row i of the activation is delayed i cycles. Zero is injected
   // when a_valid is low so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;
   genvar	   i;
   generate
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew
         wire [DW-1:0] din =
		       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};
         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;
         end
         else begin : g_skewn
            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  sr[0] <= din;
                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // The systolic array.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot;
   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_array
     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),
      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));

   //##########################################################################
   // Output de-skew: column j is delayed N-1-j cycles so all columns of a
   // result row line up. Column N-1 (longest path) needs no delay.
   //##########################################################################
   genvar j;
   generate
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew
         localparam D = N - 1 - j;
         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];
         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;
         end
         else begin : g_deskewn
            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  dr[0] <= cin;
                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[D-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed LAT compute cycles.
   //##########################################################################
   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;
   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst)
        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};
      else if (en)
        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};
   end
   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];

endmodule

[Step 2/60]
// Self-checking testbench for the tpu benchmark (8x8 weight-stationary
// systolic matmul tile). Reads vectors.dat, one line per clock cycle:
//   w_valid w_data(64b) a_valid a_data(64b)   c_valid c_data(256b)   (hex)
// The expected outputs are the ORIGINAL logikbench tpu's per-cycle behavior;
// c_valid and c_data are compared CYCLE-ACCURATELY every cycle (c_data only
// while c_valid is high — the original holds stale data between results).
module tb;
  localparam N = 8, DW = 8, ACCW = 32;
  int total_checks;
  int total_errors;

  logic clk, rst;
  logic w_valid, a_valid, c_valid;
  logic [N*DW-1:0] w_data, a_data;
  logic [N*ACCW-1:0] c_data;

  tpu dut
    (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .w_valid(w_valid), .w_data(w_data),
     .a_valid(a_valid), .a_data(a_data), .c_valid(c_valid), .c_data(c_data));

  initial clk = 0;
  always #5 clk = ~clk;

  integer fd, rc, line_num;
  string line_buf;
  logic s_wv, s_av, e_cv;
  logic [63:0] s_wd, s_ad;
  logic [255:0] e_cd;

  initial begin
    total_checks = 0;
    total_errors = 0;
    w_valid = 0; a_valid = 0; w_data = 0; a_data = 0;
    rst = 1;
    repeat (4) @(negedge clk);
    rst = 0;

    fd = $fopen("vectors.dat", "r");
    if (fd == 0) begin
      $display("ERROR: cannot open vectors.dat");
      $fatal(1);
    end
    line_num = 0;
    while (!$feof(fd)) begin
      line_num = line_num + 1;
      void'($fgets(line_buf, fd));
      if (line_buf.len() == 0) continue;
      if (line_buf.substr(0, 0) == "#") continue;
      rc = $sscanf(line_buf, "%h %h %h %h %h %h", s_wv, s_wd, s_av, s_ad, e_cv, e_cd);
      if (rc != 6) continue;
      w_valid = s_wv; w_data = s_wd; a_valid = s_av; a_data = s_ad;
      @(negedge clk);
      total_checks = total_checks + 1;
      if (c_valid !== e_cv || (e_cv && c_data !== e_cd)) begin
        $display("TB_ERROR line=%0d exp(cv=%b cd=%h) got(cv=%b cd=%h)",
                 line_num, e_cv, e_cd, c_valid, c_data);
        total_errors = total_errors + 1;
        if (total_errors > 20) begin
          $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
          $fatal(1, "FAIL (too many errors, aborting)");
        end
      end
    end
    $fclose(fd);

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
    if (total_errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

[ASSISTANT]
Now let me create an optimized version. My analysis of the baseline semantics (derived from the RTL):

- `c_data[j](Ï) = Î£_i a_i(Ï-15) Â· w_ij(Ï-15+i+j)` â the weight *sampling time* differs per depth, so I must keep the systolic stagger (this matters for mid-drain weight reloads).
- Partial sums after depth *i* hold at most *i+1* products â widths 16,17,17,18,18,18,18,19 suffice (not 32).
- Depth 0 needs no adder (product only). Deskew registers only need 19 bits.
- The horizontal activation registers can be a shared per-row delay chain (84 bytes vs 92).
  -> create_file(filename='v2_systolic.v', content='// Optimized weight-stationary systolic MXU, cycle-accurate to logikbench tpu.\n//\n// Key optimizations vs starting_point.v:\n//  * partial sums narrowed to the minimum provable width per depth\n//    (16,17,17,18,18,18,18,19 instead of 32) -> smaller adders + fewer FFs\n//  * depth 0 needs no adder (its "partial sum" is just the product)\n//  * output de-skew registers narrowed to 19 bits (result fits), sign\n//    extended to 32 bits only at the pins\n//  * activation delay network is one shared per-row shift chain with taps\n//    (drops the dead east-exit register of the mesh)\n//  * weight storage is the same per-column south-shifting register file\nmodule tpu (\n  input clk, rst, w_valid, a_valid,\n  input [63:0] w_data, a_data,\n  output c_valid,\n  output [255:0] c_data\n);\n  wire en = ~w_valid;\n\n  // ---------------- weights: per-column south-shifting registers --------\n  reg [7:0] wq [0:7][0:7];\n  integer ii, jj;\n  always @(posedge clk) begin\n    if (rst) begin\n      for (jj = 0; jj < 8; jj = jj+1)\n        for (ii = 0; ii < 8; ii = ii+1)\n          wq[ii][jj] <= 8\'h00;\n    end else if (w_valid) begin\n      for (jj = 0; jj < 8; jj = jj+1) begin\n        wq[0][jj] <= w_data[jj*8 +: 8];\n        for (ii = 1; ii < 8; ii = ii+1)\n          wq[ii][jj] <= wq[ii-1][jj];\n      end\n    end\n  end\n\n  // ---------------- activation delay network ----------------------------\n  // act[i][j] = injected row byte i delayed by (i+j) compute cycles\n  wire [7:0] act [0:7][0:7];\n  genvar gi, gj;\n  generate\n    for (gi = 0; gi < 8; gi = gi+1) begin : g_arow\n      wire [7:0] din = a_valid ? a_data[gi*8 +: 8] : 8\'h00;\n      reg [7:0] c [0:13];\n      integer q;\n      always @(posedge clk) begin\n        if (rst) begin\n          for (q = 0; q < 14; q = q+1) c[q] <= 8\'h00;\n        end else if (en) begin\n          c[0] <= din;\n          for (q = 1; q < 14; q = q+1) c[q] <= c[q-1];\n        end\n      end\n      for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_atap\n        if (gi + gj == 0) begin : g_d0\n          assign act[gi][gj] = din;\n        end else begin : g_dn\n          assign act[gi][gj] = c[gi+gj-1];\n        end\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- products ---------------------------------------------\n  wire signed [15:0] prod [0:7][0:7];\n  generate\n    for (gi = 0; gi < 8; gi = gi+1)\n      for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_prod\n        assign prod[gi][gj] = $signed(act[gi][gj]) * $signed(wq[gi][gj]);\n      end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- per-column accumulate chain --------------------------\n  generate\n    for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_col\n      reg signed [15:0] p0;\n      reg signed [16:0] p1, p2;\n      reg signed [17:0] p3, p4, p5, p6;\n      reg signed [18:0] p7;\n\n      // sign-extended products where the accumulator grows\n      wire signed [16:0] e1 = prod[1][gj];\n      wire signed [17:0] e3 = prod[3][gj];\n      wire signed [18:0] e7 = prod[7][gj];\n\n      always @(posedge clk) begin\n        if (rst) begin\n          p0 <= 16\'sd0; p1 <= 17\'sd0; p2 <= 17\'sd0; p3 <= 18\'sd0;\n          p4 <= 18\'sd0; p5 <= 18\'sd0; p6 <= 18\'sd0; p7 <= 19\'sd0;\n        end else if (en) begin\n          p0 <= prod[0][gj];\n          p1 <= p0 + e1;\n          p2 <= p1 + prod[2][gj];\n          p3 <= p2 + e3;\n          p4 <= p3 + prod[4][gj];\n          p5 <= p4 + prod[5][gj];\n          p6 <= p5 + prod[6][gj];\n          p7 <= p6 + e7;\n        end\n      end\n\n      // de-skew: column j is delayed (7-j) compute cycles\n      if (7 - gj == 0) begin : g_dd0\n        assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{p7[18]}}, p7};\n      end else begin : g_ddn\n        reg [18:0] dr [0:7-gj-1];\n        integer q;\n        wire [18:0] last;\n        always @(posedge clk) begin\n          if (rst) begin\n            for (q = 0; q < 7-gj; q = q+1) dr[q] <= 19\'h0;\n          end else if (en) begin\n            dr[0] <= p7;\n            for (q = 1; q < 7-gj; q = q+1) dr[q] <= dr[q-1];\n          end\n        end\n        assign last = dr[7-gj-1];\n        assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{last[18]}}, last};\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- valid pipeline ---------------------------------------\n  reg [14:0] vpipe;\n  always @(posedge clk) begin\n    if (rst) vpipe <= 15\'h0;\n    else if (en) vpipe <= {vpipe[13:0], a_valid};\n  end\n  assign c_valid = vpipe[14];\n\nendmodule\n')

[ASSISTANT]
Now let me implement my key optimization: splitting the multiply and add into separate pipeline cycles (with proper retiming analysis), which should roughly halve the critical path.

My timing analysis: in the original, each cycle contains mult+add (662ps of logic). By registering products separately and adding one cycle later (with a matched 1-cycle-shorter deskew), each cycle contains only a mult OR an add. For column 7 (the tightest, 0 deskew slack), the final add becomes combinational at the output.
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized weight-stationary systolic MXU, cycle-accurate to logikbench tpu.\n//\n// vs v2: multiply and add are split into separate pipeline stages (products\n// are registered in q[], then added one cycle later), so no cycle contains\n// both a multiplier and an adder. The head of each column chain gets one\n// extra pipeline register (p0) and the tail saves one deskew stage; column 7\n// (zero deskew slack) uses a combinational final add at the output pin.\nmodule tpu (\n  input clk, rst, w_valid, a_valid,\n  input [63:0] w_data, a_data,\n  output c_valid,\n  output [255:0] c_data\n);\n  wire en = ~w_valid;\n\n  // ---------------- weights: per-column south-shifting registers ----------\n  reg [7:0] wq [0:7][0:7];\n  integer ii, jj;\n  always @(posedge clk) begin\n    if (rst) begin\n      for (jj = 0; jj < 8; jj = jj+1)\n        for (ii = 0; ii < 8; ii = ii+1)\n          wq[ii][jj] <= 8'h00;\n    end else if (w_valid) begin\n      for (jj = 0; jj < 8; jj = jj+1) begin\n        wq[0][jj] <= w_data[jj*8 +: 8];\n        for (ii = 1; ii < 8; ii = ii+1)\n          wq[ii][jj] <= wq[ii-1][jj];\n      end\n    end\n  end\n\n  // ---------------- activation skew --------------------------------------\n  // act[i][j] = injected row byte i delayed by (i+j) compute cycles\n  wire [7:0] act [0:7][0:7];\n  genvar gi, gj;\n  generate\n    for (gi = 0; gi < 8; gi = gi+1) begin : g_arow\n      wire [7:0] din = a_valid ? a_data[gi*8 +: 8] : 8'h00;\n      reg [7:0] c [0:gi+6];\n      integer q;\n      always @(posedge clk) begin\n        if (rst) begin\n          for (q = 0; q <= gi+6; q = q+1) c[q] <= 8'h00;\n        end else if (en) begin\n          c[0] <= din;\n          for (q = 1; q <= gi+6; q = q+1) c[q] <= c[q-1];\n        end\n      end\n      for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_tap\n        if (gi + gj == 0) begin : g_t0\n          assign act[gi][gj] = din;\n        end else begin : g_tn\n          assign act[gi][gj] = c[gi+gj-1];\n        end\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- products (registered one cycle) -----------------------\n  wire signed [15:0] prod [0:7][0:7];\n  reg  signed [15:0] q    [0:7][0:7];\n  generate\n    for (gi = 0; gi < 8; gi = gi+1)\n      for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_mul\n        assign prod[gi][gj] = $signed(act[gi][gj]) * $signed(wq[gi][gj]);\n      end\n  endgenerate\n\n  integer di, dj;\n  always @(posedge clk) begin\n    if (rst) begin\n      for (dj = 0; dj < 8; dj = dj+1)\n        for (di = 0; di < 8; di = di+1)\n          q[di][dj] <= 16'sd0;\n    end else if (en) begin\n      for (dj = 0; dj < 8; dj = dj+1)\n        for (di = 0; di < 8; di = di+1)\n          q[di][dj] <= prod[di][dj];\n    end\n  end\n\n  // ---------------- per-column accumulation chain -------------------------\n  generate\n    for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_col\n      reg signed [15:0] p0;\n      reg signed [16:0] p1, p2;\n      reg signed [17:0] p3, p4, p5, p6;\n\n      always @(posedge clk) begin\n        if (rst) begin\n          p0 <= 16'sd0; p1 <= 17'sd0; p2 <= 17'sd0;\n          p3 <= 18'sd0; p4 <= 18'sd0; p5 <= 18'sd0; p6 <= 18'sd0;\n        end else if (en) begin\n          p0 <= q[0][gj];\n          p1 <= p0 + q[1][gj];\n          p2 <= p1 + q[2][gj];\n          p3 <= p2 + q[3][gj];\n          p4 <= p3 + q[4][gj];\n          p5 <= p4 + q[5][gj];\n          p6 <= p5 + q[6][gj];\n        end\n      end\n\n      if (gj < 7) begin : g_out_reg\n        reg signed [18:0] p7;\n        always @(posedge clk) begin\n          if (rst) p7 <= 19'sd0;\n          else if (en) p7 <= p6 + q[7][gj];\n        end\n        if (gj == 6) begin : g_d0\n          assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{p7[18]}}, p7};\n        end else begin : g_dn\n          reg [18:0] dr [0:6-gj-1];\n          integer k;\n          always @(posedge clk) begin\n            if (rst) begin\n              for (k = 0; k < 6-gj; k = k+1) dr[k] <= 19'h0;\n            end else if (en) begin\n              dr[0] <= p7;\n              for (k = 1; k < 6-gj; k = k+1) dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n            end\n          end\n          wire [18:0] last = dr[6-gj-1];\n          assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{last[18]}}, last};\n        end\n      end else begin : g_out_comb\n        wire signed [18:0] sum = p6 + q[7][gj];\n        assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{sum[18]}}, sum};\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- valid pipeline ----------------------------------------\n  reg [14:0] vpipe;\n  always @(posedge clk) begin\n    if (rst) vpipe <= 15'h0;\n    else if (en) vpipe <= {vpipe[13:0], a_valid};\n  end\n  assign c_valid = vpipe[14];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='v3_split.v')

dj < 8; dj = dj+1)\n      for (di = 0; di < 8; di = di+1) begin\n        qlo[di][dj] <= tlo[di][dj];\n        qhi[di][dj] <= thi[di][dj];\n      end\n  end\n\n  generate\n    for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_col\n      reg signed [15:0] p0;\n      reg signed [16:0] p1, p2;\n      reg signed [17:0] p3, p4, p5, p6;\n\n      // y = (qhi <<< 4) and z = qlo, sign-extended to the target width\n      wire signed [16:0] y1 = qhi[1][gj] <<< 4;\n      wire signed [16:0] z1 = qlo[1][gj];\n      wire signed [16:0] y2 = qhi[2][gj] <<< 4;\n      wire signed [16:0] z2 = qlo[2][gj];\n      wire signed [17:0] y3 = qhi[3][gj] <<< 4;\n      wire signed [17:0] z3 = qlo[3][gj];\n      wire signed [17:0] y4 = qhi[4][gj] <<< 4;\n      wire signed [17:0] z4 = qlo[4][gj];\n      wire signed [17:0] y5 = qhi[5][gj] <<< 4;\n      wire signed [17:0] z5 = qlo[5][gj];\n      wire signed [17:0] y6 = qhi[6][gj] <<< 4;\n      wire signed [17:0] z6 = qlo[6][gj];\n      wire signed [18:0] y7 = qhi[7][gj] <<< 4;\n      wire signed [18:0] z7 = qlo[7][gj];\n\n      wire signed [15:0] prod0 = (qhi[0][gj] <<< 4) + qlo[0][gj];\n\n      wire [16:0] s1 = p0 + y1 + z1;\n      wire [16:0] s2 = p1 + y2 + z2;\n      wire [17:0] s3 = p2 + y3 + z3;\n      wire [17:0] s4 = p3 + y4 + z4;\n      wire [17:0] s5 = p4 + y5 + z5;\n      wire [17:0] s6 = p5 + y6 + z6;\n\n      always @(posedge gclk) begin\n        p0 <= prod0;\n        p1 <= s1;\n        p2 <= s2;\n        p3 <= s3;\n        p4 <= s4;\n        p5 <= s5;\n        p6 <= s6;\n      end\n\n      wire signed [18:0] r7 = p6 + y7 + z7;\n\n      if (gj < 7) begin : g_out_reg\n        reg [18:0] p7;\n        always @(posedge gclk) p7 <= r7;\n        if (gj == 6) begin : g_d0\n          assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{p7[18]}}, p7};\n        end else begin : g_dn\n          reg [18:0] dr [0:6-gj-1];\n          integer k;\n          always @(posedge gclk) begin\n            dr[0] <= p7;\n            for (k = 1; k < 6-gj; k = k+1) dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n          end\n          wire [18:0] last = dr[6-gj-1];\n          assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{last[18]}}, last};\n        end\n      end else begin : g_out_comb\n        assign c_data[255-31 +: 32] = {{13{r7[18]}}, r7};\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  reg [14:0] vpipe;\n  always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n    if (rst) vpipe <= 15'h0;\n    else if (en) vpipe <= {vpipe[13:0], a_valid};\n  end\n  assign c_valid = vpipe[14];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='v10_narrowfix.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Now the big rewrite: a carry-save architecture. Key insights from my analysis:
- Column 7's depth-7 multiply is pinned at cycle Ï-1 (weight sampling), so mult+compress must share one cycle, with only the final CPA at the output pin.
- Keeping the partial sum as **two carry-save rows** removes the CPA from every per-cycle stage.
- All arithmetic works modulo 2Â¹â¹ with 19-bit vectors (result fits in 19 bits), so carry-shifting (`{c[17:0],1'b0}`) is exact.
  -> create_file(content="// v11: carry-save systolic array.\n//  * each PE: partial products of a*w (8 shifted copies of a / ~a selected by\n//    weight bits, plus one correction bit) compressed TOGETHER with the two\n//    incoming carry-save rows in a 5-level CSA tree -> (qs,qc) rows (19b).\n//    No CPA anywhere in the per-cycle path.\n//  * one 19-bit CPA per column at the end (with a full cycle of slack for\n//    columns 0..6, combinational at the pin for column 7).\n//  * all arithmetic is modulo 2^19 (the result fits in 19 bits), so the\n//    shifted-carry trick {c[17:0],1'b0} is exact.\nmodule tpu_pe_cs (\n  input gclk,\n  input [18:0] ps, pc,   // incoming carry-save rows (partial = ps + 2*pc)\n  input [7:0] a, w,      // activation (signed), weight (signed)\n  output [18:0] qs, qc\n);\n  wire signed [18:0] sa = {{11{a[7]}}, a};\n  wire signed [18:0] na = ~sa;                 // = sext(~a) = -a-1\n  wire [18:0] b  = {pc[17:0], 1'b0};           // 2*pc (mod 2^19)\n  // partial products: a*w = sum_{k=0..6} w_k*(a<<k)  +  w_7*((~a)<<7) + w_7<<7\n  wire [18:0] t0 = {19{w[0]}} & sa;\n  wire [18:0] t1 = {19{w[1]}} & (sa <<< 1);\n  wire [18:0] t2 = {19{w[2]}} & (sa <<< 2);\n  wire [18:0] t3 = {19{w[3]}} & (sa <<< 3);\n  wire [18:0] t4 = {19{w[4]}} & (sa <<< 4);\n  wire [18:0] t5 = {19{w[5]}} & (sa <<< 5);\n  wire [18:0] t6 = {19{w[6]}} & (sa <<< 6);\n  wire [18:0] t7 = {19{w[7]}} & (na <<< 7);\n  wire [18:0] tb = {19{w[7]}} & 19'h0080;      // correction bit at position 7\n\n  // level 1\n  wire [18:0] a1 = ps ^ b ^ t0;   wire [18:0] c1 = (ps & b) | (t0 & (ps | b));\n  wire [18:0] a2 = t1 ^ t2 ^ t3;  wire [18:0] c2 = (t1 & t2) | (t3 & (t1 | t2));\n  wire [18:0] a3 = t4 ^ t5 ^ t6;  wire [18:0] c3 = (t4 & t5) | (t6 & (t4 | t5));\n  wire [18:0] b1 = {c1[17:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [18:0] b2 = {c2[17:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [18:0] b3 = {c3[17:0], 1'b0};\n  // level 2\n  wire [18:0] d1 = a1 ^ b1 ^ a2;  wire [18:0] e1 = (a1 & b1) | (a2 & (a1 | b1));\n  wire [18:0] d2 = b2 ^ a3 ^ b3;  wire [18:0] e2 = (b2 & a3) | (b3 & (b2 | a3));\n  wire [18:0] f1 = {e1[17:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [18:0] f2 = {e2[17:0], 1'b0};\n  // level 3\n  wire [18:0] g1 = d1 ^ f1 ^ d2;  wire [18:0] h1 = (d1 & f1) | (d2 & (d1 | f1));\n  wire [18:0] g2 = f2 ^ t7 ^ tb;  wire [18:0] h2 = (f2 & t7) | (tb & (f2 | t7));\n  wire [18:0] i1 = {h1[17:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [18:0] i2 = {h2[17:0], 1'b0};\n  // level 4\n  wire [18:0] k1 = g1 ^ i1 ^ g2;  wire [18:0] l1 = (g1 & i1) | (g2 & (g1 | i1));\n  wire [18:0] m1 = {l1[17:0], 1'b0};\n  // level 5\n  wire [18:0] sn = k1 ^ m1 ^ i2;\n  wire [18:0] cn = (k1 & m1) | (i2 & (k1 | m1));\n\n  reg [18:0] qs, qc;\n  always @(posedge gclk) begin\n    qs <= sn; qc <= cn;\n  end\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu (\n  input clk, rst, w_valid, a_valid,\n  input [63:0] w_data, a_data,\n  output c_valid,\n  output [255:0] c_data\n);\n  wire gclk = clk & ~w_valid;\n  wire wclk = clk &  w_valid;\n  wire en   = ~w_valid;\n\n  // ---------------- weights: per-column south-shifting registers ----------\n  reg [7:0] wq [0:7][0:7];\n  integer ii, jj;\n  always @(posedge wclk) begin\n    for (jj = 0; jj < 8; jj = jj+1) begin\n      wq[0][jj] <= w_data[jj*8 +: 8];\n      for (ii = 1; ii < 8; ii = ii+1)\n        wq[ii][jj] <= wq[ii-1][jj];\n    end\n  end\n\n  // ---------------- activation skew --------------------------------------\n  wire [7:0] act [0:7][0:7];\n  genvar gi, gj;\n  generate\n    for (gi = 0; gi < 8; gi = gi+1) begin : g_arow\n      wire [7:0] din = a_valid ? a_data[gi*8 +: 8] : 8'h00;\n      reg [7:0] c [0:gi+6];\n      integer q;\n      always @(posedge gclk) begin\n        c[0] <= din;\n        for (q = 1; q <= gi+6; q = q+1) c[q] <= c[q-1];\n      end\n      for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_tap\n        if (gi + gj == 0) begin : g_t0\n          assign act[gi][gj] = din;\n        end else begin : g_tn\n          assign act[gi][gj] = c[gi+gj-1];\n        end\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- PE mesh (carry-save partial sums flow south) ----------\n  wire [18:0] ps [0:7][0:7];\n  wire [18:0] pc [0:7][0:7];\n  generate\n    for (gi = 0; gi < 8; gi = gi+1) begin : g_pe_row\n      for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_pe_col\n        tpu_pe_cs u_pe (\n          .gclk (gclk),\n          .ps   (gi == 0 ? 19'd0 : ps[gi-1][gj]),\n          .pc   (gi == 0 ? 19'd0 : pc[gi-1][gj]),\n          .a    (act[gi][gj]),\n          .w    (wq[gi][gj]),\n          .qs   (ps[gi][gj]),\n          .qc   (pc[gi][gj]));\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- final CPA + de-skew -----------------------------------\n  generate\n    for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_out\n      wire [18:0] r = ps[7][gj] + {pc[7][gj][17:0], 1'b0};\n      if (gj == 7) begin : g_comb\n        assign c_data[255 -: 32] = {{13{r[18]}}, r};\n      end else begin : g_reg\n        reg [18:0] r0;\n        always @(posedge gclk) r0 <= r;\n        if (gj == 6) begin : g_d0\n          assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{r0[18]}}, r0};\n        end else begin : g_dn\n          reg [18:0] dr [0:6-gj-1];\n          integer k;\n          always @(posedge gclk) begin\n            dr[0] <= r0;\n            for (k = 1; k < 6-gj; k = k+1) dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n          end\n          wire [18:0] last = dr[6-gj-1];\n          assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{last[18]}}, last};\n        end\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- valid pipeline ----------------------------------------\n  reg [14:0] vpipe;\n  always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n    if (rst) vpipe <= 15'h0;\n    else if (en) vpipe <= {vpipe[13:0], a_valid};\n  end\n  assign c_valid = vpipe[14];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='v11_cs.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Now the big structural change: pre-decode Booth radix-4 codes at load time, and split the multiply into [Booth mux + CSA tree] â (S,C) registers, so no cycle contains a full multiplier or a wide CPA+multiplier combo.
  -> create_file(content="// v12: Booth-coded weight-stationary systolic array.\n//  * weights are stored as pre-decoded radix-4 Booth codes (12b) computed\n//    during the load shift, so the per-cycle multiply is only a mux + XOR.\n//  * mult cycle: 4 Booth partials + correction vector compressed by a 3-level\n//    CSA tree into two rows (S,C); NO CPA in this cycle.\n//  * acc cycle: p_i = p_{i-1} + S + 2C  (one CSA + CPA), narrowed widths.\n//  * all arithmetic modulo 2^19 (result fits in 19 bits), so carry truncation\n//    is exact; per-depth moduli are respected by reading low bits only.\nmodule tpu (\n  input clk, rst, w_valid, a_valid,\n  input [63:0] w_data, a_data,\n  output c_valid,\n  output [255:0] c_data\n);\n  wire gclk = clk & ~w_valid;\n  wire wclk = clk &  w_valid;\n  wire en   = ~w_valid;\n\n  // radix-4 Booth codes of a weight byte:\n  //   [3g+0]=one_g, [3g+1]=two_g, [3g+2]=neg_g  (group g covers w[2g+1:2g-1])\n  function [11:0] bcode;\n    input [7:0] w;\n    begin\n      bcode[0]  = w[0];\n      bcode[1]  = w[1] & ~w[0];\n      bcode[2]  = w[1];\n      bcode[3]  = w[2] ^ w[1];\n      bcode[4]  = (w[3] & ~w[2] & ~w[1]) | (~w[3] & w[2] & w[1]);\n      bcode[5]  = w[3];\n      bcode[6]  = w[4] ^ w[3];\n      bcode[7]  = (w[5] & ~w[4] & ~w[3]) | (~w[5] & w[4] & w[3]);\n      bcode[8]  = w[5];\n      bcode[9]  = w[6] ^ w[5];\n      bcode[10] = (w[7] & ~w[6] & ~w[5]) | (~w[7] & w[6] & w[5]);\n      bcode[11] = w[7];\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  reg [11:0] bc [0:7][0:7];\n  integer ii, jj;\n  always @(posedge wclk) begin\n    for (jj = 0; jj < 8; jj = jj+1) begin\n      bc[0][jj] <= bcode(w_data[jj*8 +: 8]);\n      for (ii = 1; ii < 8; ii = ii+1)\n        bc[ii][jj] <= bc[ii-1][jj];\n    end\n  end\n\n  // ---------------- activation skew --------------------------------------\n  wire [7:0] act [0:7][0:7];\n  genvar gi, gj;\n  generate\n    for (gi = 0; gi < 8; gi = gi+1) begin : g_arow\n      wire [7:0] din = a_valid ? a_data[gi*8 +: 8] : 8'h00;\n      reg [7:0] c [0:gi+6];\n      integer q;\n      always @(posedge gclk) begin\n        c[0] <= din;\n        for (q = 1; q <= gi+6; q = q+1) c[q] <= c[q-1];\n      end\n      for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_tap\n        if (gi + gj == 0) begin : g_t0\n          assign act[gi][gj] = din;\n        end else begin : g_tn\n          assign act[gi][gj] = c[gi+gj-1];\n        end\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- PE: Booth partials + CSA tree -> (S,C) ----------------\n  reg [18:0] S [0:7][0:7];\n  reg [17:0] C [0:7][0:7];\n  generate\n    for (gi = 0; gi < 8; gi = gi+1) begin : g_prow\n      for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_pcol\n        wire [7:0]  a  = act[gi][gj];\n        wire [11:0] cd = bc[gi][gj];\n        wire [15:0] sa  = {{8{a[7]}}, a};\n        wire [15:0] sa2 = {sa[14:0], 1'b0};\n        // magnitude mux (one/two are mutually exclusive), negation via XOR\n        wire [15:0] m0 = (sa  & {16{cd[0]}}) | (sa2 & {16{cd[1]}});\n        wire [15:0] m1 = (sa  & {16{cd[3]}}) | (sa2 & {16{cd[4]}});\n        wire [15:0] m2 = (sa  & {16{cd[6]}}) | (sa2 & {16{cd[7]}});\n        wire [15:0] m3 = (sa  & {16{cd[9]}}) | (sa2 & {16{cd[10]}});\n        wire [15:0] p0 = m0 ^ {16{cd[2]}};\n        wire [15:0] p1 = m1 ^ {16{cd[5]}};\n        wire [15:0] p2 = m2 ^ {16{cd[8]}};\n        wire [15:0] p3 = m3 ^ {16{cd[11]}};\n        // 19-bit rows\n        wire [18:0] e0 = {{3{p0[15]}}, p0};\n        wire [18:0] e1 = {{3{p1[15]}}, p1, 2'b00};\n        wire [18:0] e2 = {{3{p2[15]}}, p2, 4'b0};\n        wire [18:0] e3 = {{3{p3[15]}}, p3, 6'b0};\n        wire [18:0] cr = {11'b0, cd[11], 1'b0, cd[8], 1'b0, cd[5], 1'b0, cd[2]};\n        // CSA tree 5 -> 2\n        wire [18:0] x1 = e0 ^ e1 ^ cr;\n        wire [18:0] y1 = (e0 & e1) | (cr & (e0 | e1));\n        wire [18:0] b1 = {y1[17:0], 1'b0};\n        wire [18:0] x2 = x1 ^ b1 ^ e2;\n        wire [18:0] y2 = (x1 & b1) | (e2 & (x1 | b1));\n        wire [18:0] b2 = {y2[17:0], 1'b0};\n        wire [18:0] x3 = x2 ^ b2 ^ e3;\n        wire [18:0] y3 = (x2 & b2) | (e3 & (x2 | b2));\n        always @(posedge gclk) begin\n          S[gi][gj] <= x3;\n          C[gi][gj] <= y3[17:0];\n        end\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- per-column accumulate chain ---------------------------\n  generate\n    for (gj = 0; gj < 8; gj = gj+1) begin : g_acc\n      reg [15:0] p0;\n      reg [16:0] p1, p2;\n      reg [17:0] p3, p4, p5, p6;\n\n      wire [15:0] c2_0 = {C[0][gj][14:0], 1'b0};\n      wire [16:0] c2_1 = {C[1][gj][15:0], 1'b0};\n      wire [16:0] c2_2 = {C[2][gj][15:0], 1'b0};\n      wire [17:0] c2_3 = {C[3][gj][16:0], 1'b0};\n      wire [17:0] c2_4 = {C[4][gj][16:0], 1'b0};\n      wire [17:0] c2_5 = {C[5][gj][16:0], 1'b0};\n      wire [17:0] c2_6 = {C[6][gj][16:0], 1'b0};\n      wire [18:0] c2_7 = {C[7][gj][17:0], 1'b0};\n\n      always @(posedge gclk) begin\n        p0 <= S[0][gj][15:0] + c2_0;\n        p1 <= {p0[15], p0}    + S[1][gj][16:0] + c2_1;\n        p2 <= {p1[16], p1}    + S[2][gj][16:0] + c2_2;\n        p3 <= {p2[16], p2}    + S[3][gj][17:0] + c2_3;\n        p4 <= {p3[17], p3}    + S[4][gj][17:0] + c2_4;\n        p5 <= {p4[17], p4}    + S[5][gj][17:0] + c2_5;\n        p6 <= {p5[17], p5}    + S[6][gj][17:0] + c2_6;\n      end\n\n      wire [18:0] r7 = {p6[17], p6} + S[7][gj][18:0] + c2_7;\n\n      if (gj < 7) begin : g_out_reg\n        reg [18:0] p7;\n        always @(posedge gclk) p7 <= r7;\n        if (gj == 6) begin : g_d0\n          assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{p7[18]}}, p7};\n        end else begin : g_dn\n          reg [18:0] dr [0:6-gj-1];\n          integer k;\n          always @(posedge gclk) begin\n            dr[0] <= p7;\n            for (k = 1; k < 6-gj; k = k+1) dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n          end\n          wire [18:0] last = dr[6-gj-1];\n          assign c_data[gj*32 +: 32] = {{13{last[18]}}, last};\n        end\n      end else begin : g_out_comb\n        assign c_data[255-31 +: 32] = {{13{r7[18]}}, r7};\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---------------- valid pipeline ----------------------------------------\n  reg [14:0] vpipe;\n  always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n    if (rst) vpipe <= 15'h0;\n    else if (en) vpipe <= {vpipe[13:0], a_valid};\n  end\n  assign c_valid = vpipe[14];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='v12_booth.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Now the big structural change: a **merged PE** (Booth + 4 CSA levels, carry-save state flowing down the column, no per-cycle CPA at all). This removes the 2368 mult-register FFs and the 56 accumulator CPAs; only one CPA per column at the bottom (with slack, except col 7 which is combinational at the pin).
  -> create_file('{"content":"// v14: merged-PE carry-save systolic array with Booth-decoded weights.\\n//  * weights stored as pre-decoded radix-4 Booth codes (12b), shifted south\\n//    exactly           assign act[gi][gj] = c[gi+gj-1];
        end
      end
    end
  endgenerate

  // ---------------- PE: Booth partials + CSA tree -> (S,C) ----------------
  reg [18:0] S [0:7][0:7];
  reg [17:0] C [0:7][0:7];
  generate
    for (gi = 0; gi < 8; gi = gi+1) begin : g_prow

        wire [15:0] sa2 = {sa[14:0], 1'b0};
        // magnitude mux (one/two are mutually exclusive), negation via XOR
        wire [15:0] m0 = (sa  & {16{cd[0]}}) | (sa2 & {16{cd[1]}});
        wire [15:0] m1 = (sa  & {16{cd[3]}}) | (sa2 & {16{cd[4]}});
        wire [15:0] m2 = (sa  & {16{cd[6]}}) | (sa2 & {16{cd[7]}});
        wire [15:0] m3 = (sa  & {16{cd[9]}}) | (sa2 & {16{cd[10]}});
        wire [15:0] p0 = m0 ^ {16{cd[2]}};
        wire [15:0] p1 = m1 ^ {16{cd[5]}};
        wire [15:0] p2 = m2 ^ {16{cd[8]}};
        wire [15:0] p3 = m3 ^ {16{cd[11]}};
        // 19-bit rows
        wire [18:0] e0 = {{3{p0[15]}}, p0};
        wire [18:0] e1 = {{3{p1[15]}}, p1, 2'b00};
        wire [18:0] e2 = {{3{p2[15]}}, p2, 4'b0};
        wire [18:0] e3 = {{3{p3[15]}}, p3, 6'b0};
        wire [18:0] cr = {11'b0, cd[11], 1'b0, cd[8], 1'b0, cd[5], 1'b0, cd[2]};
        // CSA tree 5 -> 2
        wire [18:0] x1 = e0 ^ e1 ^ cr;
        wire [18:0] y1 = (e0 & e1) | (cr & (e0 | e1));
        wire [18:0] b1 = {y1[17:0], 1'b0};
        wire [18:0] x2 = x1 ^ b1 ^ e2;
        wire [18:0] y2 = (x1 & b1) | (e2 & (x1 | b1));
        wire [18:0] b2 = {y2[17:0], 1'b0};

[ASSISTANT]
Now let me implement a faster accumulate stage using an explicit Sklansky prefix adder (abc's default `+` mapping gives ~413ps; a prefix structure should be ~245ps), plus a shallower multiply stage (2 CSA levels, keeping the sparse Booth-correction row as a 4-bit register).

+ 1) begin : gl\n      for (i = 0; i < W; i = i + 1) begin : gi\n        if (((i >> l) & 1) != 0) begin : blk\n          assign Gg[l+1][i] = Gg[l][i] | (Pp[l][i] & Gg[l][i - (1 << l)]);\n [ASSISTANT]
Found the real bug: my prefix adder used a Kogge-Stone partner index (`i - 2^l`) with a Sklansky-style condition, leaving bit ranges uncovered. The correct Sklansky partner is the *top bit of the lower half*. Fixing:
  -> edit_file(filename='v15_peacc.v', new_string='    for (l = 0; l < LV; l = l + 1) begin : gl\n      for (i = 0; i < W; i = i + 1) begin : gb\n        if (((i >> l) & 1) != 0) begin : blk\n          // partner = top bit of the lower half of the 2^(l+1) block\n          localparam integer J = (((i >> (l+1)) << (l+1)) + (1 << l) - 1);\n          assign Gg[l+1][i] = Gg[l][i] | (Pp[l][i] & Gg[l][J]);\n          assign Pp[l+1][i] = Pp[l][i] & Pp[l][J];\n        end else begin : pass\n          assign Gg[l+1][i] = Gg[l][i];\n          assign Pp[l+1][i] = Pp[l][i];\n        end\n      end\n    end', old_string='    for (l = 0; l < LV; l = l + 1) begin : gl\n      for (i = 0; i < W; i = i + 1) begin : gb\n        if (((i >> l) & 1) != 0) begin : blk\n          assign Gg[l+1][i] = Gg[l][i] | (Pp[l][i] & Gg[l][i - (1 << l)]);\n          assign Pp[l+1][i] = Pp[l][i] & Pp[l][i - (1 << l)];\n        end else begin : pass\n          assign Gg[l+1][i] = Gg[l][i];\n          assign Pp[l+1][i] = Pp[l][i];\n        end\n      end\n    end')

## Summary

**Best design: `v12_booth.v`** — cost **1,628,389** (area 3475, delay 468.6ps @ target_delay=400), vs. 4,624,409 for the provided `starting_point.v` (2.8× better).

### Progression of what worked
| Step | Cost | Change |
|---|---|---|
| starting_point | 4.62M | baseline PE mesh |
| `v2_systolic.v` | 3.21M | narrowed accumulators (16â19b per depth instead of 32b), depth-0 without adder, 19b deskew, shared per-row activation delay chain |
| `v3_split.v` | 2.71M | split multiply and accumulate into separate cycles (products registered, then added one cycle later; deskew shortened by 1 to compensate) |
| `v5_splitmult.v` | 2.50M | split 8Ã8 multiply into two 8Ã4 multiplies, recombined via CSA folded into the accumulate step |
| `v8_gatedclk.v` | 1.90M | **gated clocks** (`gclk = clk & ~w_valid`, `wclk = clk & w_valid`) replacing ~3700 per-FF enable muxes |
| `v10_narrowfix.v` | 1.83M | re-narrowed accumulators (with correct sign extension) |
| `v12_booth.v` | **1.63M** | **Booth radix-4**: weights stored as pre-decoded 12-bit Booth codes in the weight shift registers; multiply cycle = mux+XOR+CSA(5â2); accumulate cycle = CSA+CPA |

### Highest-impact optimizations
1. **Gated clocks** (biggest single win): eliminated all enable muxes on the data paths.
2. **Booth radix-4 with pre-decoded weights**: the weight load shift register stores Booth codes, so the per-cycle multiply is just a magnitude mux + XOR-negate + CSA tree â no general multiplier.
3. **Pipeline splitting** of multiply vs. accumulate (with matched deskew reduction), enabled by deriving the exact per-cycle dataflow: `c_data[j](Ï) = Î£_i a_i(Ïâ15)Â·w_ij(Ïâ15+i+j)`.
4. **Width narrowing**: per-depth accumulator widths 16/17/17/18/18/18/18/19 and 19-bit deskew registers (result provably fits in 19 bits; all arithmetic mod 2Â¹â¹ with truncated-carry shifting is exact).
5. **Reset removal** on data arrays (only the 15-bit valid pipe resets) â safe because `c_valid` gates every data comparison and the pipes are structurally flushed.

### What didn't work / regressions
- **Fully carry-save accumulate chain** (`v13_csbooth.v`, 1.72M): no CPA per cycle, but the 7-input compression in the PE was both larger and slower than v12's split.
- **Sklansky prefix adders** (`v15`, `v17`, ~1.83M): abc's default `+` mapping beat my hand-built prefix adders in both area and delay.
- **Folding the Booth correction row into the multiply stage** (`v16`, 1.84M): smaller area but pushed delay up.
- Several outright bugs caught by the vectors: zero-extension via concatenation when narrowing widths, an unused declared signal (`pc2`) that silently dropped a CSA row, Booth correction bits at wrong bit positions, and a wrong Sklansky partner index.

### Lessons
- Deriving the exact cycle-by-cycle dataflow equation first made aggressive retiming/narrowing safe and easy to verify.
- Synthesis tool behavior (abc's adder mapping, clock-gating inference) matters as much as RTL structure â hand-optimized arithmetic often lost to the default mapping.
- Reset removal and clock gating are huge area levers when the protocol guarantees structural flush and the output is valid-gated.
- I'd budget more steps for exploring around v12 (e.g., different Booth groupings, hybrid CSA depths per column, and target_delay sweeps), since several late variants were within ~10% and close variants of the winner often pay off.
